I guess… the main reason I came in is that I’ve been having these thoughts that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. Not like I’ve sat down and made some plan to hurt myself or anything like that—I haven’t. It’s more this thought that comes into my head, especially when I’m feeling worn down, like just not having to face everything would be a relief.

I know that probably sounds serious, and it scares me a little to even say it out loud. I didn’t want to ignore it or pretend it wasn’t happening, so I figured I should come in and talk to someone before it gets any worse.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s hard to pin down exactly, honestly. It hasn’t been like one single day where I woke up and thought, “Okay, now I’m having this thought.” It’s more like I started catching myself thinking it here and there—that it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up—and then I realized it was happening enough that it scared me.

I haven’t made any plan to hurt myself. I want to be clear about that. It’s not like I’ve sat there working out how I’d do something. It’s more this tired, worn-down thought that comes in, like not waking up would mean I wouldn’t have to deal with everything. And I guess the fact that I keep having it is what made me think I shouldn’t just brush it off.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… that’s changed. I mean, I used to have little things that kind of brought me back down or made the week feel normal, you know? Like fishing used to settle me. Even just getting the gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go—that usually gave me something to look forward to.

And I’ve always liked digging through vinyl records, too. I could spend an hour flipping through crates and not even notice the time. Tailgating, same thing—being around people, getting the food ready, all that. And I have this home-brew I’d check on, and normally I’d be kind of excited to see how it was coming along.

Lately, though, all of that just feels like… work. Like chores. I don’t get that same little lift from any of it. I’ll think, “Maybe I should go fishing,” and then it just sounds like too much effort. Or I’ll look at the records and not care enough to put one on. It’s not that I hate those things now, exactly. It’s more like I quit caring, and that bothers me because those were the things that usually helped me feel like myself.

Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t sit the same, and I catch myself having to pull them up more than usual.

It’s mostly that I’m just not eating like I normally do. Most days lately I kind of have to remind myself, like, “You should probably eat something.” And then supper will be sitting there and it just looks like too much trouble, even if it’s something I’d normally be fine with. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.

I mean, I used to have little things that kind of brought me back down or made the week feel normal, you know? Like fishing used to settle me. Even just getting the gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go—that usually gave me something to look forward to.\n\nAnd I’ve always liked digging through vinyl records, too. I could spend an hour flipping through crates and not even notice the time. Tailgating, same thing—being around people, getting the food ready, all that. And I have this home-brew I’d check on, and normally I’d be kind of excited to see how it was coming along.\n\nLately, though, all of that just feels like… work. Like chores. I don’t get that same little lift from any of it. I’ll think, “Maybe I should go fishing,” and then it just sounds like too much effort. Or I’ll look at the records and not care enough to put one on. It’s not that I hate those things now, exactly. It’s more like I quit caring, and that bothers me because those were the things that usually helped me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t sit the same, and I catch myself having to pull them up more than usual.\n\nIt’s mostly that I’m just not eating like I normally do. Most days lately I kind of have to remind myself, like, “You should probably eat something.” And then supper will be sitting there and it just looks like too much trouble, even if it’s something I’d normally be fine with. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.

My stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.

And then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. I’ll just lay there watching the room get lighter, thinking the same things over and over. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.

Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.

Like I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.

It’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.

I’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.

So yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.

My usual routines at home got sloppy enough that I knew I could not just handle it by willpower.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 3 weeks\n  manifestation: That high-energy, out-of-control stretch lasted about three weeks after I was fired.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 3 months\n  manifestation: The low spell has been hanging on for about three months since that crash.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 54 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S032\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.\n\nMy stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.\n\nAnd then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. I’ll just lay there watching the room get lighter, thinking the same things over and over. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.

Like fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.

Same with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.

Even tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. And I’ve got a home-brew I should be checking on, and that used to be kind of fun, watching the process and fussing with it. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.

So yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.

It’s been… about three months, I’d say. Maybe a little over, but three months is the number that keeps coming to mind.

It kind of started after that crash, if that makes sense. Like after I came down from that stretch where I was all wound up and trying to do everything at once. Since then, it’s just been hanging around. At first I thought, okay, I’m just tired, I’ll bounce back in a week or two. But then weeks kept going by, and fishing still felt like work, the records still just sat there, everything I normally enjoy started feeling like another obligation.

So yeah… this low spell has been going on for roughly three months now. It hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.

Even just getting the gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go—that usually gave me something to look forward to.\n\nAnd I’ve always liked digging through vinyl records, too. I could spend an hour flipping through crates and not even notice the time. Tailgating, same thing—being around people, getting the food ready, all that. And I have this home-brew I’d check on, and normally I’d be kind of excited to see how it was coming along.\n\nLately, though, all of that just feels like… work. Like chores. I don’t get that same little lift from any of it. I’ll think, “Maybe I should go fishing,” and then it just sounds like too much effort. Or I’ll look at the records and not care enough to put one on. It’s not that I hate those things now, exactly. It’s more like I quit caring, and that bothers me because those were the things that usually helped me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t sit the same, and I catch myself having to pull them up more than usual.\n\nIt’s mostly that I’m just not eating like I normally do. Most days lately I kind of have to remind myself, like, “You should probably eat something.” And then supper will be sitting there and it just looks like too much trouble, even if it’s something I’d normally be fine with. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.\n\nMy stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.\n\nAnd then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. I’ll just lay there watching the room get lighter, thinking the same things over and over. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. And I’ve got a home-brew I should be checking on, and that used to be kind of fun, watching the process and fussing with it. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.\n\nSo yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. Maybe a little over, but three months is the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt kind of started after that crash, if that makes sense. Like after I came down from that stretch where I was all wound up and trying to do everything at once. Since then, it’s just been hanging around. At first I thought, okay, I’m just tired, I’ll bounce back in a week or two. But then weeks kept going by, and fishing still felt like work, the records still just sat there, everything I normally enjoy started feeling like another obligation.\n\nSo yeah… this low spell has been going on for roughly three months now. Yeah… there was one thing. I almost didn’t want to bring it up because it sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud.

When I was in that wound-up stretch, I was sitting alone in my kitchen — not at work, not anywhere near the store — and I heard my old store manager’s voice. Clear as day, like it was coming over an intercom, the way announcements sound in a store. And he said, “move the display.”

It wasn’t like I was remembering him saying it, either. I know what that feels like, like a memory or an annoying thought. This was more like I actually heard it, like my head snapped a little because I thought, why am I hearing that here? I was just sitting there in the kitchen. No TV on, nobody else there.

It only made sense in that weird retail way, because I’ve heard that kind of thing a million times at work, managers telling you to shift something around, move merchandise, fix an endcap, whatever. But this wasn’t at work, and it was his voice specifically. It really unsettled me. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.

So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. And I’ve got a home-brew I should be checking on, and that used to be kind of fun, watching the process and fussing with it. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.\n\nSo yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. Maybe a little over, but three months is the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt kind of started after that crash, if that makes sense. Like after I came down from that stretch where I was all wound up and trying to do everything at once. Since then, it’s just been hanging around. At first I thought, okay, I’m just tired, I’ll bounce back in a week or two. But then weeks kept going by, and fishing still felt like work, the records still just sat there, everything I normally enjoy started feeling like another obligation.\n\nSo yeah… this low spell has been going on for roughly three months now. It hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange sensations, heard voices, or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was one thing. I almost didn’t want to bring it up because it sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud.\n\nWhen I was in that wound-up stretch, I was sitting alone in my kitchen — not at work, not anywhere near the store — and I heard my old store manager’s voice. Clear as day, like it was coming over an intercom, the way announcements sound in a store. And he said, “move the display.”\n\nIt wasn’t like I was remembering him saying it, either. I know what that feels like, like a memory or an annoying thought. This was more like I actually heard it, like my head snapped a little because I thought, why am I hearing that here? I was just sitting there in the kitchen. No TV on, nobody else there.\n\nIt only made sense in that weird retail way, because I’ve heard that kind of thing a million times at work, managers telling you to shift something around, move merchandise, fix an endcap, whatever. But this wasn’t at work, and it was his voice specifically. It really unsettled me. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced any other unusual perceptions or heard any other voices since that incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 54 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Hallucinations\nmanifestation: \"When I was all wound up, I heard my old store manager's voice clear as day over an intercom telling me to 'move the display,' even when I was sitting alone in my kitchen.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really other ones that I can point to like that. That one with my old manager’s voice is the main thing — the one that sticks in my head because it was so clear and so out of place.

I was all wound up then, and I was just sitting alone in my kitchen, and it sounded like him over the store intercom telling me to “move the display.” Clear as day. Not like a thought, not like I was remembering work. It felt like I heard it.

Since then, I can’t say I’ve had another voice like that, or seen anything, at least not that I’m aware of. That incident is really the one that shook me.

I didn’t want to ignore it or pretend it wasn’t happening, so I figured I should come in and talk to someone before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to pin down exactly, honestly. It hasn’t been like one single day where I woke up and thought, “Okay, now I’m having this thought.” It’s more like I started catching myself thinking it here and there—that it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up—and then I realized it was happening enough that it scared me.\n\nI haven’t made any plan to hurt myself. I want to be clear about that. It’s not like I’ve sat there working out how I’d do something. It’s more this tired, worn-down thought that comes in, like not waking up would mean I wouldn’t have to deal with everything. I mean, I used to have little things that kind of brought me back down or made the week feel normal, you know? Like fishing used to settle me. Even just getting the gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go—that usually gave me something to look forward to.\n\nAnd I’ve always liked digging through vinyl records, too. I could spend an hour flipping through crates and not even notice the time. Tailgating, same thing—being around people, getting the food ready, all that. And I have this home-brew I’d check on, and normally I’d be kind of excited to see how it was coming along.\n\nLately, though, all of that just feels like… work. Like chores. I don’t get that same little lift from any of it. I’ll think, “Maybe I should go fishing,” and then it just sounds like too much effort. Or I’ll look at the records and not care enough to put one on. It’s not that I hate those things now, exactly. It’s more like I quit caring, and that bothers me because those were the things that usually helped me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t sit the same, and I catch myself having to pull them up more than usual.\n\nIt’s mostly that I’m just not eating like I normally do. Most days lately I kind of have to remind myself, like, “You should probably eat something.” And then supper will be sitting there and it just looks like too much trouble, even if it’s something I’d normally be fine with. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.\n\nMy stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.\n\nAnd then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. I’ll just lay there watching the room get lighter, thinking the same things over and over. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.\n\nSo yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. My usual routines at home got sloppy enough that I knew I could not just handle it by willpower.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 3 weeks\n  manifestation: That high-energy, out-of-control stretch lasted about three weeks after I was fired.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 3 months\n  manifestation: The low spell has been hanging on for about three months since that crash.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 54 / retail_salesperson / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S032\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or heard any other voices since that incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: No, not really other ones that I can point to like that. Not like a thought, not like I was remembering work. It felt like I heard it.\n\nSince then, I can’t say I’ve had another voice like that, or seen anything, at least not that I’m aware of. I’m not really sure how to answer that one. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed myself moving slower in the sense of, like, other people saying I’m walking slow or talking slow or anything like that. Nobody’s pointed it out to me.

So I guess I’d say I haven’t clearly noticed moving slower than usual. If anything, it’s more that I can delay starting things, but I’m not sure how much of that counts as what you’re asking.

I didn’t want to ignore it or pretend it wasn’t happening, so I figured I should come in and talk to someone before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to pin down exactly, honestly. I mean, I used to have little things that kind of brought me back down or made the week feel normal, you know? Like fishing used to settle me. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.\n\nMy stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.\n\nAnd then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. I’ll just lay there watching the room get lighter, thinking the same things over and over. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. And I’ve got a home-brew I should be checking on, and that used to be kind of fun, watching the process and fussing with it. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.\n\nSo yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. Maybe a little over, but three months is the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt kind of started after that crash, if that makes sense. Like after I came down from that stretch where I was all wound up and trying to do everything at once. Since then, it’s just been hanging around. At first I thought, okay, I’m just tired, I’ll bounce back in a week or two. But then weeks kept going by, and fishing still felt like work, the records still just sat there, everything I normally enjoy started feeling like another obligation.\n\nSo yeah… this low spell has been going on for roughly three months now. It hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange sensations, heard voices, or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was one thing. I almost didn’t want to bring it up because it sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud.\n\nWhen I was in that wound-up stretch, I was sitting alone in my kitchen — not at work, not anywhere near the store — and I heard my old store manager’s voice. Clear as day, like it was coming over an intercom, the way announcements sound in a store. And he said, “move the display.”\n\nIt wasn’t like I was remembering him saying it, either. I know what that feels like, like a memory or an annoying thought. This was more like I actually heard it, like my head snapped a little because I thought, why am I hearing that here? I was just sitting there in the kitchen. No TV on, nobody else there.\n\nIt only made sense in that weird retail way, because I’ve heard that kind of thing a million times at work, managers telling you to shift something around, move merchandise, fix an endcap, whatever. But this wasn’t at work, and it was his voice specifically. It really unsettled me. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or heard any other voices since that incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: No, not really other ones that I can point to like that. That one with my old manager’s voice is the main thing — the one that sticks in my head because it was so clear and so out of place.\n\nI was all wound up then, and I was just sitting alone in my kitchen, and it sounded like him over the store intercom telling me to “move the display.” Clear as day. Not like a thought, not like I was remembering work. It felt like I heard it.\n\nSince then, I can’t say I’ve had another voice like that, or seen anything, at least not that I’m aware of. That incident is really the one that shook me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or having trouble getting started on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one. Nobody’s pointed it out to me.\n\nGetting started on things… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. It’s hard to tell from the inside, you know?\n\nSo I guess I’d say I haven’t clearly noticed moving slower than usual. Yeah, I have, actually. Not like pacing in a dramatic way all day, but I’ve had this thing where I just can’t seem to stay with one thing long enough.

I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. And I’ve got a home-brew I should be checking on, and that used to be kind of fun, watching the process and fussing with it. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.\n\nSo yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. Maybe a little over, but three months is the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt kind of started after that crash, if that makes sense. Like after I came down from that stretch where I was all wound up and trying to do everything at once. Since then, it’s just been hanging around. At first I thought, okay, I’m just tired, I’ll bounce back in a week or two. But then weeks kept going by, and fishing still felt like work, the records still just sat there, everything I normally enjoy started feeling like another obligation.\n\nSo yeah… this low spell has been going on for roughly three months now. It hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange sensations, heard voices, or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was one thing. I almost didn’t want to bring it up because it sounds ridiculous when I say it out loud.\n\nWhen I was in that wound-up stretch, I was sitting alone in my kitchen — not at work, not anywhere near the store — and I heard my old store manager’s voice. Clear as day, like it was coming over an intercom, the way announcements sound in a store. And he said, “move the display.”\n\nIt wasn’t like I was remembering him saying it, either. I know what that feels like, like a memory or an annoying thought. This was more like I actually heard it, like my head snapped a little because I thought, why am I hearing that here? I was just sitting there in the kitchen. No TV on, nobody else there.\n\nIt only made sense in that weird retail way, because I’ve heard that kind of thing a million times at work, managers telling you to shift something around, move merchandise, fix an endcap, whatever. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or heard any other voices since that incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: No, not really other ones that I can point to like that. That one with my old manager’s voice is the main thing — the one that sticks in my head because it was so clear and so out of place.\n\nI was all wound up then, and I was just sitting alone in my kitchen, and it sounded like him over the store intercom telling me to “move the display.” Clear as day. Not like a thought, not like I was remembering work. It felt like I heard it.\n\nSince then, I can’t say I’ve had another voice like that, or seen anything, at least not that I’m aware of. That incident is really the one that shook me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or having trouble getting started on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one. I mean… I don’t think I’ve noticed myself moving slower in the sense of, like, other people saying I’m walking slow or talking slow or anything like that. Nobody’s pointed it out to me.\n\nGetting started on things… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like I’ll sit there longer than I should before doing something, kind of staring at it or thinking about how I should do it, but I don’t know if that’s “slower” exactly or just me dragging my feet. It’s hard to tell from the inside, you know?\n\nSo I guess I’d say I haven’t clearly noticed moving slower than usual. If anything, it’s more that I can delay starting things, but I’m not sure how much of that counts as what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have, actually. Not like pacing in a dramatic way all day, but I’ve had this thing where I just can’t seem to stay with one thing long enough.\n\nI’ll sit down meaning to do something simple, like work on job applications, and then suddenly I’m sketching out ideas for woodworking projects, then I’m opening up my resume and rewriting parts of it, then I’m back looking at postings again. It’s like I keep bouncing from one thing to the next before I’ve really finished the first one.\n\nAnd I’ll think, okay, just sit here and finish this application, just do that one thing, but then I’m up getting a pencil, or checking something, or moving papers around, or starting another idea because it feels important right then. That one with my old manager’s voice is the main thing — the one that sticks in my head because it was so clear and so out of place.\n\nI was all wound up then, and I was just sitting alone in my kitchen, and it sounded like him over the store intercom telling me to “move the display.” Clear as day. Not like a thought, not like I was remembering work. It felt like I heard it.\n\nSince then, I can’t say I’ve had another voice like that, or seen anything, at least not that I’m aware of. That incident is really the one that shook me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or having trouble getting started on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one. Nobody’s pointed it out to me.\n\nGetting started on things… maybe sometimes, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Like I’ll sit there longer than I should before doing something, kind of staring at it or thinking about how I should do it, but I don’t know if that’s “slower” exactly or just me dragging my feet. It’s hard to tell from the inside, you know?\n\nSo I guess I’d say I haven’t clearly noticed moving slower than usual. If anything, it’s more that I can delay starting things, but I’m not sure how much of that counts as what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have, actually. Not like pacing in a dramatic way all day, but I’ve had this thing where I just can’t seem to stay with one thing long enough.\n\nI’ll sit down meaning to do something simple, like work on job applications, and then suddenly I’m sketching out ideas for woodworking projects, then I’m opening up my resume and rewriting parts of it, then I’m back looking at postings again. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Most of the day… I’d say on the days it’s happening, yeah, it takes up a lot of the day. It’s not like I’m literally standing up and pacing from breakfast until bedtime, but I keep not being able to stay planted on one thing.

Like I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I’m going to do job applications, that’s the responsible thing, just get through a couple of them. And then before I know it, I’m sketching some woodworking idea on a scrap of paper, then I’m back on the computer messing with my resume, rewriting a sentence that probably didn’t need rewriting, then I’m looking at job postings again, then I’m up moving papers around or grabbing something. It’s this bouncing around, over and over.

So yeah, it’s not every single second, but it’s been enough of the day that I notice I’m hardly sitting still long enough to actually finish one thing before I’m starting the next.

It’s more this thought that comes into my head, especially when I’m feeling worn down, like just not having to face everything would be a relief.\n\nI know that probably sounds serious, and it scares me a little to even say it out loud. I didn’t want to ignore it or pretend it wasn’t happening, so I figured I should come in and talk to someone before it gets any worse.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: It’s hard to pin down exactly, honestly. It hasn’t been like one single day where I woke up and thought, “Okay, now I’m having this thought.” It’s more like I started catching myself thinking it here and there—that it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up—and then I realized it was happening enough that it scared me.\n\nI haven’t made any plan to hurt myself. I want to be clear about that. It’s not like I’ve sat there working out how I’d do something. It’s more this tired, worn-down thought that comes in, like not waking up would mean I wouldn’t have to deal with everything. And I guess the fact that I keep having it is what made me think I shouldn’t just brush it off.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s changed. I mean, I used to have little things that kind of brought me back down or made the week feel normal, you know? Like fishing used to settle me. Even just getting the gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go—that usually gave me something to look forward to.\n\nAnd I’ve always liked digging through vinyl records, too. I could spend an hour flipping through crates and not even notice the time. Tailgating, same thing—being around people, getting the food ready, all that. I don’t get that same little lift from any of it. I’ll think, “Maybe I should go fishing,” and then it just sounds like too much effort. Or I’ll look at the records and not care enough to put one on. It’s not that I hate those things now, exactly. It’s more like I quit caring, and that bothers me because those were the things that usually helped me feel like myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. Like I’ll put them on and they don’t sit the same, and I catch myself having to pull them up more than usual.\n\nIt’s mostly that I’m just not eating like I normally do. Most days lately I kind of have to remind myself, like, “You should probably eat something.” And then supper will be sitting there and it just looks like too much trouble, even if it’s something I’d normally be fine with. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.\n\nMy stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.\n\nAnd then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. I’ll just lay there watching the room get lighter, thinking the same things over and over. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people. And I’ve got a home-brew I should be checking on, and that used to be kind of fun, watching the process and fussing with it. Now it just feels like something else I’m behind on.\n\nSo yeah, the things that usually settle me down have started feeling more like chores than anything I actually look forward to.\n\nDoctor: How long have you felt like the things you used to enjoy are now feeling like chores?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about three months, I’d say. Maybe a little over, but three months is the number that keeps coming to mind.\n\nIt kind of started after that crash, if that makes sense. Like after I came down from that stretch where I was all wound up and trying to do everything at once. Since then, it’s just been hanging around. At first I thought, okay, I’m just tired, I’ll bounce back in a week or two. But then weeks kept going by, and fishing still felt like work, the records still just sat there, everything I normally enjoy started feeling like another obligation.\n\nSo yeah… this low spell has been going on for roughly three months now. It hasn’t really lifted the way I thought it would.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange sensations, heard voices, or seen things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was one thing. I know what that feels like, like a memory or an annoying thought. This was more like I actually heard it, like my head snapped a little because I thought, why am I hearing that here? No TV on, nobody else there.\n\nIt only made sense in that weird retail way, because I’ve heard that kind of thing a million times at work, managers telling you to shift something around, move merchandise, fix an endcap, whatever. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or heard any other voices since that incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: No, not really other ones that I can point to like that. No TV on, nobody else there.\n\nIt only made sense in that weird retail way, because I’ve heard that kind of thing a million times at work, managers telling you to shift something around, move merchandise, fix an endcap, whatever. But this wasn’t at work, and it was his voice specifically. It really unsettled me. I didn’t see anything, but that voice… yeah, that happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or heard any other voices since that incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: No, not really other ones that I can point to like that. That one with my old manager’s voice is the main thing — the one that sticks in my head because it was so clear and so out of place.\n\nI was all wound up then, and I was just sitting alone in my kitchen, and it sounded like him over the store intercom telling me to “move the display.” Clear as day. Not like a thought, not like I was remembering work. It felt like I heard it.\n\nSince then, I can’t say I’ve had another voice like that, or seen anything, at least not that I’m aware of. That incident is really the one that shook me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or having trouble getting started on things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure how to answer that one. Like I’ll sit there longer than I should before doing something, kind of staring at it or thinking about how I should do it, but I don’t know if that’s “slower” exactly or just me dragging my feet. It’s hard to tell from the inside, you know?\n\nSo I guess I’d say I haven’t clearly noticed moving slower than usual. If anything, it’s more that I can delay starting things, but I’m not sure how much of that counts as what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have, actually. It’s like I keep bouncing from one thing to the next before I’ve really finished the first one.\n\nAnd I’ll think, okay, just sit here and finish this application, just do that one thing, but then I’m up getting a pencil, or checking something, or moving papers around, or starting another idea because it feels important right then. I don’t weigh myself every day or anything, so I can’t give you an exact number, but I’ve noticed my jeans are fitting looser. Most days lately I kind of have to remind myself, like, “You should probably eat something.” And then supper will be sitting there and it just looks like too much trouble, even if it’s something I’d normally be fine with. I’ll look at it and feel like chewing it, cleaning up after, all of it is just more than I want to deal with. So I’ll pick at it or leave it. I guess that’s probably why my clothes feel looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any problems with sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sleep’s been… not great. I mean, I do sleep some, but it doesn’t feel like real rest. It’s like even when I manage to fall asleep, I’ll wake up before daylight, way earlier than I need to, and I’m just instantly tense.\n\nMy stomach will already be tight, like that knotted-up feeling, and my mind starts running before I’m even fully awake. It goes straight to bills, what I’m behind on, what I messed up, what I should’ve done differently. There’s no slow waking up or lying there comfortable. It’s like I open my eyes and I’m already in trouble somehow.\n\nAnd then once that starts, I don’t really drift back off. So yeah… I’m sleeping, but it doesn’t feel like it’s doing me much good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or feeling like you're speeding up and can't calm down?\n\nPatient: Yeah… more like speeding up, I guess. Not so much talking slow or moving slow. There have been times where I just can’t seem to sit with one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I need to fill out some job applications, and I’ll get maybe partway through one, then suddenly I’m thinking I should sketch out that woodworking project I had in my head. So I’ll grab paper and start drawing measurements or shelves or whatever, and then before that’s done I’m back at the computer rewriting my resume, changing little words around, trying to make it sound better. Then I’ll remember another application, or I’ll think of some other project, and I’m up again.\n\nIt’s not like I’m getting a ton finished, either. That’s the frustrating part. I’m moving around and switching gears so much that I hardly sit still long enough to complete one thing before I’m already onto the next. It feels busy, but not exactly productive.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything in particular lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic, but after those times where I’m sort of running on this burst of energy, like I’m trying to do ten things and convince myself I’m finally going to fix everything, when that wears off it’s like somebody just pulled the plug out of me.\n\nI’ll end up sitting at the kitchen table with coffee in front of me, and I’m not even really drinking it. I’m just staring at it, and everything feels kind of hollow. And then the shame comes in. Like, what was I doing, why can’t I just act normal, why do I start things and not finish them, why do I make everything harder than it has to be. It’s not always one big thing I did, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I’ve let myself down somehow, or that I should be doing better at my age.\n\nSo yeah, guilty… ashamed, mostly. Sitting there feeling empty and kind of embarrassed with myself.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… that’s actually one of the things that’s bothered me, because I usually do have stuff that kind of resets me.\n\nLike fishing, for example. Normally even just getting my gear together, checking the line, thinking about where I might go, that would calm me down a little. Lately it just feels like too much effort. I’ll look at the tackle box and think, “I should organize that,” and then it just feels like another job sitting there.\n\nSame with my records. I’ve got vinyl I used to love digging through, putting something on, reading the sleeve, all that. Now I’ll stand there flipping through them and nothing really grabs me. It’s not that I hate it, it’s just… flat. Like the part of me that usually cares isn’t showing up.\n\nEven tailgating, which I used to look forward to, feels like planning and packing and dealing with people.
