It’s hard to tell from the inside, you know?\n\nSo I guess I’d say I haven’t clearly noticed moving slower than usual. If anything, it’s more that I can delay starting things, but I’m not sure how much of that counts as what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have, actually. Not like pacing in a dramatic way all day, but I’ve had this thing where I just can’t seem to stay with one thing long enough.\n\nI’ll sit down meaning to do something simple, like work on job applications, and then suddenly I’m sketching out ideas for woodworking projects, then I’m opening up my resume and rewriting parts of it, then I’m back looking at postings again. It’s like I keep bouncing from one thing to the next before I’ve really finished the first one.\n\nAnd I’ll think, okay, just sit here and finish this application, just do that one thing, but then I’m up getting a pencil, or checking something, or moving papers around, or starting another idea because it feels important right then. So yeah… trouble sitting still, or at least trouble settling down into one thing, that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of restlessness and inability to sit still most of the day?\n\nPatient: Most of the day… I’d say on the days it’s happening, yeah, it takes up a lot of the day. It’s not like I’m literally standing up and pacing from breakfast until bedtime, but I keep not being able to stay planted on one thing.\n\nLike I’ll sit down thinking, okay, I’m going to do job applications, that’s the responsible thing, just get through a couple of them. And then before I know it, I’m sketching some woodworking idea on a scrap of paper, then I’m back on the computer messing with my resume, rewriting a sentence that probably didn’t need rewriting, then I’m looking at job postings again, then I’m up moving papers around or grabbing something. It’s this bouncing around, over and over.\n\nSo yeah, it’s not every single second, but it’s been enough of the day that I notice I’m hardly sitting still long enough to actually finish one thing before I’m starting the next.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so, though I haven’t been weighing myself every day or anything like that, so I don’t know the exact number. It’s more that I’ve noticed my jeans are looser, like I’m having to pull them up more than usual, and that’s not really normal for me.\n\nMost days lately I kind of have to remind myself to eat. It’s not even that I’m trying to skip meals on purpose. It’s more like supper will be there and I’ll look at it and it just seems like too much trouble, like the plate is more than I want to deal with. I might take a few bites and then just leave it sitting there.\n\nSo, significant… I don’t know what counts as significant, but yes, I’ve definitely been eating less, and my clothes fit looser.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other hallucinations or heard any voices other than your old store manager's voice since that incident?\n\nPatient: No, not other voices, not that I can think of. It was really that one thing with my old store manager’s voice, and that’s what made it so strange, because it was so specific.\n\nI was sitting alone in my kitchen, not at the store or anything, and I was all wound up, like my mind was going fast, and then I heard him clear as day like it was coming over an intercom. The kind of voice you’d hear at work, you know, giving some instruction. He said to move the display, and it was so familiar that for a second I almost reacted like I was back there.\n\nBut other than that particular incident, I don’t think I’ve been hearing other people talking or anything like that. That one just really stuck with me because it felt so real in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other hallucinations since the incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: Other than that one with my old store manager, no… at least nothing else that I’d call a hallucination. That was the only time it was that clear.\n\nIt wasn’t like a vague thought or me remembering his voice, either. I was sitting alone in my kitchen, and I was already kind of wound up, and then I heard him clear as day, like it was coming over the store intercom. He said to “move the display,” just like he would’ve said at work. It was so specific and familiar that for a second I almost felt like I needed to get up and do it.\n\nBut no, I haven’t been seeing things or hearing other voices besides that. That one incident is the one that stuck out because it felt real in the moment.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other hallucinations or delusions since the incident with your old store manager's voice?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. The store manager’s voice was the only thing like that, where I actually heard it and it felt outside of me, like it was happening in the room.\n\nAnd the only time I had that… that really strong belief, I guess, where I was just completely sure I could walk into any store and take it over, like I knew better than everyone and could run the whole place, that was during those same wired weeks after I got fired. At the time it didn’t feel strange to me. It felt obvious, like everyone else just hadn’t caught up yet.\n\nBut then when I kind of crashed down afterward, all of that stopped. I didn’t keep hearing him, and I didn’t still feel so certain about the store thing. Mostly I just felt embarrassed, honestly. Like, I’d look back on what I was thinking and feel ashamed that I had been so convinced.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts of not wanting to wake up are interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think they have, more than I wanted to admit at first. It wasn’t like I just had a bad morning and then got on with things. When I was having those thoughts about not wanting to wake up, everything started slipping.\n\nI fell behind on bills because I’d see them sitting there and just not deal with them, or I’d tell myself I’d do it later and then days would go by. I missed callbacks and appointments too, which is not like me when I’m doing okay. And I needed to be looking for work, I knew that, but I couldn’t keep a steady job search going. I’d start, maybe look at a couple postings, then just kind of shut down or put it off.\n\nEven stuff at home got sloppy. My usual routines, dishes, laundry, keeping track of things… it all got away from me enough that I knew it wasn’t just laziness or me needing to “try harder.” I kept thinking I should be able to handle it by willpower, but I really wasn’t handling it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're a burden to your friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if “burden” is the exact word I would’ve used right away, but it’s pretty close to how it felt. After that burst of energy I’d had wore off, it was like somebody just pulled the plug on me. I went from thinking I could handle everything to just… sitting there.\n\nI’d sit at the kitchen table in the morning with my coffee, and I wouldn’t even really drink it sometimes. I’d just stare at it, feeling empty and ashamed. And when I feel like that, it’s hard not to think people around me are probably tired of dealing with me, or disappointed in me. Even if they weren’t saying that, I kind of assumed it.\n\nSo yes, I guess I did feel like I was weighing people down. Not in a dramatic way every second, but in this heavy, quiet way, like I’d made a mess of things and other people had to see it."
